# handsoff — hardware acceptance checklist

Run this on the real desktop (Arch/CachyOS + niri + Yeti mic + speakers)
after any deployment, and especially after installer, audio, or
desktop-action changes. Every line is meant to be executed, not read.
Nothing here can be fully automated — that is what `tests/` is for.

Legend: ☐ pending · ✅ pass · ❌ fail (note why below the line)
Verified-by codes: [auto] scriptable end-to-end on this machine ·
[human] needs ears/eyes/hands at the desk.

## 1. Deployment trust (P0)

- ✅ [auto] `./install.sh` completes; `~/.config/handsoff/deployment.json`
  exists with a row for **every** shipped file — the five top-level entries
  (`handsoff.py`, `handsoff-settings.py`, `handsoff-restart`,
  `settings_schema.py`, `hardware.py`) plus every `core/*.py` module, all
  `source==installed: True`. The core set is generated by glob over the
  staged tree, so a newly added module cannot be left behind (re-verified
  2026-09-11: 15 rows, `core/theme.py` among them).
  Re-verified 2026-09-09
  after the ff2f158 run (per-package pacman probe + unit-name loop fired live).
- ✅ [auto] `--ptt doctor` prints
  `deployment: in-sync — installed copy matches the checkout` (2026-09-09)
- ✅ [auto] `--ptt health` includes a `"deployment"` section with
  `status: in-sync` (2026-09-09)
- ✅ [auto] Settings health bar shows `deploy: ok` — verified at the data
  layer: the live bubble health snapshot fed through the Settings app's own
  `_fmt_health()` renders
  `mic: … · brain: ok gemma4:latest · tts/stt: ok · deploy: ok`
  (the string the bar displays verbatim — `gemma4:latest` was the
  configured value on the test box; the shipped default is `qwen3:8b`; pixel-OCR of the Qt window was
  attempted but multi-output screenshots made OCR unreliable)
- ✅ [auto] `systemctl --user status handsoff` → active; unit has
  `Restart=always` (2026-09-09)

## 2. Startup, restart, crash recovery, shutdown (P0)

- ✅ [auto] Fresh start: stop → `systemctl --user start handsoff` → unit
  active, `--ptt status` answers `state=idle handsfree=off model=gemma4:latest`
  (`gemma4:latest` was the configured value on the test box; default `qwen3:8b`)
  (2026-09-09)
- ✅ [auto] Restart: `~/.local/bin/handsoff-restart` → exactly one bubble
  after the swap. **Found and fixed a real race today**: against a
  systemd-owned bubble the script's manual TERM/KILL pass triggered
  `Restart=always` auto-spawn before its own `systemctl restart` (transient
  double bubble, journal 16:39:13–16:39:15). The script now goes systemd-first
  and only kills manually when no unit is managing the bubble. Retest: PASS.
- ✅ [auto] Crash recovery: SEGV on the main pid → systemd restarts within
  ~5 s; doctor mentions the crash log; previous run (15:08–15:09) additionally
  showed the spoken short report and `crash.log` truncated-not-unlinked,
  same inode, mode 0600.
- ✅ [auto] Crash-loop guard: 5+ rapid SEGVs → unit entered `failed` with
  journal `Start request repeated too quickly`; `systemctl --user reset-failed
  handsoff` + start recovered it to active (2026-09-09)
- ☐ [human] Shutdown: end the graphical session → bubble exits cleanly
  (`PartOf=graphical-session.target`), no zombie after re-login. Not
  automatable from inside the session (it *is* the session).
  Auto half verified 2026-09-18: the unit carries
  `PartOf=graphical-session.target` and `WantedBy=graphical-session.target`,
  and `systemctl --user stop` leaves it `inactive` with **no surviving bubble
  process**. Still human: the logout itself — after re-login,
  `systemctl --user is-active handsoff` should read `active` and
  `pgrep -c -f "python3 /home/quinton/.local/bin/handsoff.py"` should print 1.

## 3. Yeti capture (P0/P1)

- ☐ [human+auto] Settings → Voice: the Yeti appears in *Input device* and can
  be selected. Enumeration verified 2026-09-09: `Blue Microphones: USB Audio
  (hw:4,0)` is visible to sounddevice (Blue = Yeti vendor), **but the
  currently configured input is the Logitech StreamCam** — the Yeti needs to
  be actually selected in Settings → Voice.
  Auto half verified 2026-09-18, and the 2026-09-09 note is now DEAD: running
  the exact sequence `refresh_mics()` runs (20 devices, 14 of them inputs) the
  Yeti is listed as `Blue Microphones: USB Audio (hw:4,0)` **and the saved
  `mic_device` resolves to combo index 3** — the combo's `findData` matches it,
  so it is selected rather than falling back to "System default" at index 0.
  `mic_device` itself now reads the Yeti, and `pactl get-default-source` is the
  Yeti too (`alsa_input.usb-Generic_Blue_Microphones_2045BAB07KW8-00…`), so no
  Settings change is needed for the Yeti to be what the bubble listens to.
  Look at: Settings → Voice → *Input device* — the dropdown should read
  `Blue Microphones: USB Audio (hw:4,0)`, not "System default".
- ☐ [human] Live test: level meter follows the room; speech passes threshold
  Auto half verified 2026-09-18: during a real PTT capture `--ptt level`
  reported `{"raw": 0.79…1.0, "ui": …, "source": "ptt", "state":
  "listening"}` and fell to `raw 0.149` in the pause — the meter tracks speech
  in both directions; the same verb reports `source: mic` while the listener
  runs and `source: tts` while the bubble speaks. Threshold logic is proven in
  the journal in both directions: `capture rejected (frames=1024 peak=311
  threshold=500)` (historic, quiet room) and today's capture accepted
  (`frames=148236 rate=44100` → whisper ran).
  Look at: Settings → Voice → the meter while you speak — the bar should move
  with your voice and sit flat when you stop.
- ☐ [human] Push-to-talk round trip: hold → speak → release; transcript correct
  Auto half verified 2026-09-18 end to end (loopback): a piper utterance of
  "What time is it?" played into the room while `--ptt start` held the mic gave
  `ptt timing: stop_ms=45 submit_ms=1 frames=148236 rate=44100`, whisper
  processed 9.265 s (VAD removed 6.353 s of silence), `heard (gen=4)` with
  `echo-check: heard … -> False`, and the turn answered `saying: It is
  currently 9:37 PM on Friday, September 18, 2026.` `history.json` gained
  exactly those two messages — `user | What time is it?` and the reply — so the
  transcript was correct AND the exchange persisted (see the 2026-09-18 row
  for the memory defect that used to swallow it).
  Look at: hold the key, ask something, release — your own words should appear
  in Settings → History as a `user` turn.
- ☐ [human] Hands-free wake: say the wake name; engagement window opens
  Auto half verified 2026-09-18: with hands-free on, a piper "Cypher." played
  into the room produced `wake word — engaged for 45.0s`, and a fragment a
  second earlier produced `ignored (no wake word)` — both halves of the gate.
  The spotter also loads its stock set (`['alexa','hey_jarvis','hey_mycroft',
  'timer','weather']`) with `spotter_models` empty, and the transcript gate
  accepts the configured name. The follow-up question in that run was lost to
  overlap (`ignored (unintelligible while engaged)`) because the bubble was
  still speaking; the window itself had opened.
  Look at: say "cypher" (or "hey cypher") with hands-free on, then ask
  something WITHOUT the wake name — the answer should come. Journal proof:
  `wake word — engaged for 45.0s`.
- ☐ [human] Stop echo rejection: while it speaks a long reply, say "stop" →
  quiet, no self-transcription
  Attempted 2026-09-18 and the run was INVALID, not the code: hands-free was
  off, so nothing was listening while the bubble spoke and the spoken "Stop."
  could not be heard (it kept speaking). The code route is measured —
  `--ptt interrupt` during a reply truncated it to 0.05 s of audible speech
  with `state=idle` after — so what remains untested is the VOICE route to it.
  Look at: hands-free ON (or hold PTT), start a long reply, say "stop" while it
  speaks — it should go quiet mid-sentence, and its own words must not appear
  in the transcript.
- ☐ [human] Mic self-heal: unplug the Yeti hands-free → degraded state
  journaled/spoken, recovers on replug (or capped retries)
  Not automatable — it needs hands on the cable, and the Yeti is the default
  source as well as the configured one. The healthy side of its ledger is on
  record from today's runs (`opens_ok=0 opens_failed=0 heal=0`, transitions
  journaled as `mic health: state changed (start) -> stopped`), and the
  degraded side is covered in the suite.
  Look at: hands-free on, unplug the Yeti → expect a journaled/spoken degraded
  state and no crash; replug → recovery, or capped retries.

## 4. TTS (P0/P1)

- ✅ [human+auto, synthesis verified] Replies spoken with the configured voice;
  no echo into the transcript. Engine verified 2026-09-12 on chatterbox-turbo:
  `tts_to_wav` writes 24 kHz 16-bit PCM, and with a reference clip set the
  conditionals provably change the audio (same sentence, clone vs built-in:
  waveform correlation +0.015, f0 138 Hz vs 218 Hz against the clip's 110 Hz).
  Audible playback + echo-rejection need ears.
  Auto half verified 2026-09-18: with Chrome muted so the room was clean, a
  `--ptt say` of a 70-character sentence was measurably PLAYED — the sink
  monitor showed speech at peak amplitude 0.646 / peak rms 0.208 against a
  floor of 0.019, ~30× the floor — and `history.json` did not change while it
  spoke, so the bubble's own voice never entered the conversation.
  Listen for: the reply in the configured voice, and then check Settings →
  History holds nothing attributed to you that you did not say.
  Verified 2026-09-21 18:16 at the machine: the reply was HEARD, clearly, in the
  cloned voice — the one clause no monitor can judge, since a muted or
  unplugged sink looks identical to a working one from the app's side (the
  sink was checked first: unmuted, 35%, active port analog-output, and
  `tts_volume` 1.0, so the path to the ears was clear). On the machine side,
  `history.json` stayed BYTE-IDENTICAL (6 entries, sha `c8e711419b42fa1d`)
  across 34s of OPEN MICROPHONE while the bubble spoke a 161-character sentence
  containing its own wake word ("Hey Jarvis…"), and again across a
  262-character one — so the transcript holds nothing the bubble said.
  Two limits on that echo evidence, stated rather than rounded off: the
  captures that were refused were stopped by the WAKE-WORD rule (`ignored (no
  wake word)`), not by the echo guard — the guard answered "not my own speech"
  (False) on both — and a third utterance that arrived INSIDE the 6s follow-up
  window (opened 18:13:06.136, decided 18:13:11.937) was also refused for want
  of a wake word. That is either a sub-second window edge or the window not
  being honoured; one sample cannot tell them apart, so it is recorded as an
  open question in `specs/90-audit.md` rather than counted as a pass.
  This item was only testable after a capture-device change: the default source
  (Blue Microphones / Yeti) opens and then NEVER returns a frame
  (`opens_failed` 0→5, `RuntimeError` on every reopen, `frames=0`,
  `stalled=true`), while the StreamCam opens at 16 kHz and delivers audio
  (32 000 frames, peak 0.2975, `utterances=1`). `mic_device` is now
  `Logitech StreamCam: USB Audio (hw:3,0)`, set the way Settings → Voice sets
  it (same key, same plain-device-name value, applied by `reload-settings` with
  no restart); the previous value (`""`) is kept at
  `/tmp/settings.json.before-4x`. This is also the §3 finding: the Yeti is
  absent from `sounddevice`'s device list entirely (it appears only as an
  OUTPUT, `Blue Microphones: USB Audio (hw:4,0)`, `in=0`), so "the Yeti appears
  in *Input device*" is not merely untested — on this tree it cannot appear.
- ☐ [human] Barge-in: click the bubble while it speaks → playback stops
  instantly (needs the bubble audibly speaking; supervised test)
  Auto half verified 2026-09-18 except the click itself: `--ptt interrupt`
  DURING a reply truncated it (the monitor counted 3.8 s of audible speech out
  of a far longer reply, then `state=idle`), against a control with no
  interrupt where the whole reply was spoken. The click sends the same verb.
  Look at: click the bubble mid-sentence — the sound should stop immediately.
  Machine half re-measured 2026-09-21 on these bytes, closed loop and clean:
  the monitor was read live and the interrupt was sent only once speech had
  been audible for 3.0s, so no wall-clock or model-load guess entered the
  number. A 300-character announcement whose natural length is 20s (control,
  unbroken, 18:27) and 17s (a click aimed 340 px wide of the bubble — a sham
  control) stopped after 4.3s of speech: the interrupt truncates, and
  `state=idle` follows.
  NOT literally instant, and the item's wording is therefore not met to the
  letter: with Chrome and speech-dispatcher muted so only the bubble was on
  the sink, ~1.0–1.2s of speech-level audio followed the interrupt (chunks at
  0.06–0.12 rms, with a dip and a partial resume before silence). `play_wav`
  checks its cancel event every `_PLAY_BLOCK` = 1024 frames (~43ms at 24 kHz),
  so a one-second tail is longer than the player's own granularity — recorded
  as an open finding, not rounded off.
  The CLICK half could not be settled by machine and is left to the hand:
  `core/bubble.py:3924` calls `interrupt()` on PRESS, before the 140ms
  hold-to-record timer — the right verb, in the right order — but the bubble
  sets `Qt.WindowDoesNotAcceptFocus` (core/bubble.py:1978), so a click cannot
  be confirmed by a focus change, and on this desktop neither niri's
  `tile_pos_in_workspace_view` (2963,1310) nor the window rule's bottom-right
  anchor (2995,1651) produced a PTT capture when pressed — a programmatic
  click cannot be aimed at the bubble here. Supervising it remains the test.
- ☐ [human] Volume/rate sliders in Settings take effect after Apply
  Auto half measured 2026-09-18 through the same path Apply uses
  (`reload-settings`): `tts_volume` 0.25 vs 1.0 moved the monitor's speech
  level (peak rms 0.131 vs 0.214, peak amplitude 0.42 vs 0.82) and `tts_rate`
  0.75 vs 1.5 moved the spoken duration (12.97 s vs 3.41 s of sound for the
  same sentence). Both settings were restored afterwards. The direction is
  proven; the exact scaling is not, because the rate change also moves
  sentence splitting (measured ratio 3.8 against a nominal 2.0).
  Listen/see: move Volume and Speech rate in Settings → Voice, Apply, then use
  the Test button — the sentence should get quieter or louder, slower or
  faster.

## 5. ydotool / typing (P1)

One-pass re-check: `python ~/.local/bin/handsoff.py --ptt selftest` runs the
whole section (daemon, focus plumbing, terminal refusal, type_text landing,
clipboard round-trip) against self-launched scratch windows and restores the
clipboard. First live run 2026-09-09: `verdict: PASS (5/5 checks)`.

- ✅ [auto] ydotoold running (Arch user unit: `ydotool.service`; other
  distros: `ydotoold.service`). Verified 2026-09-09: daemon reachable at
  `/run/user/1000/.ydotool_socket`, doctor reports
  `ydotool: ok (daemon reachable …)`. The installer now enables the right
  unit (`ydotool.service` first) — confirmed in the ff2f158 install run.
- ✅ [auto] Type into a focused editor → text lands in the app (2026-09-09):
  `type_text "handsoff ydotool e2e 2026-09-09"` into gnome-text-editor →
  "typed 31 chars into org.gnome.TextEditor"; full-screen OCR read the exact
  string back off the screen.
- ✅ [auto] Typing into a *focused terminal* is refused (2026-09-09): foot
  focused → `type_text` AND `press_keys` both returned
  "REFUSED: the focused window is a terminal (foot)"; nothing was executed.
  Fail-closed on unknown focus is unit-tested in tests/test_desktop.py.
- ✅ [auto] `press_keys "ctrl+c"` copies from the focused app (2026-09-09):
  ctrl+a then ctrl+c in gnome-text-editor → `wl-paste` returned the exact
  file content byte-for-byte.

## 6. niri IPC & desktop actions (P1)

- ✅ [auto] `run_command "niri msg --json windows"` works (2026-09-09)
- ✅ [auto] `niri_capabilities` lists this niri version's actions, not a
  hardcoded set (2026-09-09)
- ✅ [auto] open_app launches, identifies and focuses: `open_app foot` →
  window appeared, `is_focused: true` (2026-09-09)
- ✅ [auto] Move to workspace: `workspace move "texteditor to 2"` →
  `moved the window to workspace 2`; window verified on workspace idx 2.
  (Note: `workspace_id` in niri's JSON is a global id, NOT the user-facing
  index — verify against `niri msg --json workspaces` idx, as this run did.)
- ✅ [auto] Polite close confirmed gone: `close_window "texteditor"` →
  "closed 1 window(s): … (confirmed gone)", window absent afterwards
- ✅ [auto] scroll scrolls (`scrolled down 2 notch(es)` with the operator
  permission enabled the legitimate way — wheel via ydotool) and `wait`
  settles (2026-09-09)
- ☐ [human, supervised] Operator element clicks: `screen_elements` →
  `click_element` clicks the right element; pointer scale correct on a
  fractional-scale output. Left supervised: a mis-aimed click on the real
  desktop could hit unrelated windows.
  Auto half verified 2026-09-18 via the DEPLOYED `core/tools.py` against the
  live desktop: `screen_elements` returned 23 elements with screen coordinates
  and `pointer scale 1.25` — the fractional-scale case this item is about —
  and `click_element` resolves a scanned number, converting screen→pointer
  coordinates by that scale.
  The LANDING could not be signed off, and the reason is a finding of its own:
  a throwaway window with a self-logging button was pressed **11 times in 3
  seconds** (22:28:15–22:28:18) with no hand on the mouse and no `ydotool`
  invocation from the test, and an idle probe window then saw a lone
  `MouseButtonRelease` with no press. Something on this desktop delivers
  phantom input — see the 2026-09-18 row — which is also the leading
  explanation for the "settings changed themselves" observations.
  Look at: run `screen_elements`, then `click_element` on an element you can
  watch, and confirm the pointer lands where the scan said.

## 9. How to close the remaining 13 (the walk-through)

Every automatable line in sections 1–8 has been run and is recorded above; the
13 ☐ items are all `[human]`, and they need the machine, not more code. This
section is the order that closes them with the least sitting-down, what to
LISTEN FOR on each, and the one line of evidence to add afterwards. Nothing here
needs a code change.

**Step 0 — two things to put right first (5 minutes, once).**

1. **Plug the Yeti back in.** It is not on the bus as of 2026-09-19 (inputs are
the StreamCam at `hw:3,0` and a Creative SB at `hw:0,0`), and section 3 cannot
be attempted without it. When it appears, check `arecord -l` — the card index
will NOT be 4 any more — and set it in Settings → Voice. The stale pin
(`… (hw:4,0)`) has been cleared to "system default" for you; the bubble now
FALLS BACK to the default and says so instead of failing when a pinned device
is absent, so the difference between a working mic and a dead one is what it
records, not whether it opens at all. `--ptt health` carries
`mic.fallback: true` while it is standing in for a missing pin, and doctor's mic
line names the device it actually opened.
2. **Merge the niri window rule** (item reprinted by the installer):
   `~/.config/handsoff/niri-window-rule.kdl` → `~/.config/niri/config.kdl`, then
   `niri msg action reload-config`. Verified 2026-09-19: the live config has
   ZERO `window-rule` blocks, so the bubble is currently laid out as an ordinary
tiled window.

**Sitting A — at the desk, no reboot (`## 3` × 6, `## 4` × 3, `## 6` × 1).**
Each line is: do this → expect this → say this back to me.

- **3 · level meter**: open it in Settings → Voice, then talk, then stop. The
  level bar must FOLLOW the room and fall when you stop. A bar that sits still
  while you talk is the failure this item exists to catch.
- **3 · PTT round trip**: hold the key, say "what time is it", release. Expect a
  transcript that matches the words, then a spoken answer. Record `ptt timing:`
  from the journal (`stop_ms`, `frames`, `rate`).
- **3 · hands-free wake**: turn hands-free on, say the wake name, then speak
  WITHOUT touching anything. Expect the engagement window to open (the bubble's
  state shows listening) and the utterance to be handled.
- **3 · stop echo rejection**: while a LONG reply is playing, say "stop". Expect
  playback to stop and the words not to appear as a new turn — the bubble must
  not answer its own voice.
- **3 · mic self-heal**: with hands-free on, unplug the Yeti. Expect a degraded
  state (`--ptt health` → `state: open-failing`, `mic.fallback: true` once the
  default stands in, and a notification the first time), then REPLUG it and
expect recovery without a restart. Record the two timestamps.
- **4 · replies audible**: ask for something short. Expect the configured voice
  (chatterbox-turbo, `optimus_clip.wav`), intelligible, no clicks or truncation.
  Doctor must read `tts: chatterbox-turbo … model loaded`.
- **4 · barge-in**: click the bubble while it is speaking. Expect playback to
  stop immediately, mid-sentence.
- **4 · sliders apply after Apply**: move the volume and rate sliders, press
  Apply, then have it speak. Expect an audible change WITHOUT a restart; the
  journal should show `settings reloaded live (no restart)`.
- **6 · operator element clicks**: run `screen_elements` (the control socket's
  diagnostic), then have it click a named element in a scratch window you do not
  mind losing. Expect the click to land on that element and only that one. This
  one needs `ydotool` live (`ydotoold` is running) and the Super-chord gate to
  NOT fire, because the chord is only sent when the compositor really holds it.

**Sitting B — the session enders (each needs one thing you cannot fake).**

- **7 · autostart is exactly once**: reboot, then `systemctl --user status
  handsoff` and `pgrep -af handsoff.py`. Expect ONE process and ONE unit, and no
  second copy from niri's `spawn-at-startup` (systemd owns autostart now; the
  installer says so). Record the PID count after login.
- **8 · session teardown**: log out and back in. Expect hands-free to come back
  OFF (it is OFF now, so OFF is "unchanged") and any pending reminder to have
  survived — set one for +20 minutes before logging out. `reminders.json` is the
evidence.
- **2 · shutdown is clean**: end the graphical session with the bubble running.
  Expect it to exit rather than be killed mid-turn, and no `Fatal Python error`
  in `journalctl --user -u handsoff` for that shutdown.

**What to say back, and what gets recorded.** One line per item in the
sign-off table below, in this shape: which item, what you saw or heard, and the
timestamp. A failure is as useful as a pass — the three failures this session
got out of a real turn (the unusable pinned mic, the PTT failure that never
reached `opens_failed`, the niri rule never merged) were all found by RUNNING
it, and all three are now fixed or named. After the list is closed, the project
is complete by its own definition: every automatable line green on a clean
checkout of HEAD, the bubble deployed in-sync, and every human line either
checked with a date or explicitly abandoned with a reason.

| 2026-09-12 | Buffy (live redeploy) | uncommitted third-review-batch set (running == checkout == installed, `hardware.py` sha256 632e863d…, `core/tools.py` 02115f70…) | A third, narrower batch: **two real shared-state races, one test-isolation defect that could fail CI for unrelated reasons**, four checked and deliberately left alone. *Races*: (1) `hardware._probe`'s check-then-store on the TTL cache ran unsynchronized while **both** `doctor`'s worker thread and the Qt thread's hardware tick read and write the same dict, so a stamp could be written from a superseded value — a stale entry coming back looking freshly probed; a module-level `_TTL_LOCK` now guards both, deliberately **not** held across `fn()` (the probers shell out to `nvidia-smi`/`systemctl`/`journalctl` and serializing them would let one stalled probe block every section — two threads may still probe the same section, last result wins, which is what the lock needs to prevent). (2) `if not job._announced: job._announced = True` was a lock-free check-then-set and a spoken turn can poll concurrently with a hands-free one, so a finished job was **announced twice**; replaced by `claim_announcement()`, true for exactly one caller. *Isolation*: `tests/conftest.py` loaded the monolith by path without registering it in `sys.modules`, so a later `import handsoff` built a **second instance** with its own `SETTINGS`/locks/caches — inert in practice (core submodules really are shared: `H.ToolBelt is core.tools.ToolBelt`), but exactly the shape that yields order-dependent flakes instead of errors; `_load` now registers the module (aliasing the bare name only for `handsoff.py`, since doing it for all modules would put `core/audio.py` in `sys.modules` as `"audio"`) and unwinds on a failed load. And the `TestKillProcess` pair pinned as a known flake earlier is **fixed at its cause**: it matched `sleep` by name against the real `/proc` scan while `kill_process` needs an EXACT single match, so one stray `sleep` — a leftover suite, another test's helper, the developer's shell — turned the offer into a "several match" ERROR and failed it. Reproduced deterministically with a single stray; discovery is now pinned to the test's own child (the kill still happens for real) and the real-scan behaviour keeps its own coverage. *Checked, not changed*: item 10 is **not reproducible** — a dead DGRAM server returns `ECONNREFUSED (111)` from `connect()`, so `ydotoold` liveness was never falsely reported, and the proposed `/proc`-inode check would have broken a root-owned daemon; `install.sh`'s `PYTHON_PKGS` probe already tells the user to refresh; `_resample_to_16k`'s unwindowed FFT is inaudible quality-only; `core/__init__.py`'s bare-name swap really is gated and restored. Both race guards were verified to fail on the pre-fix code. Deployed (`in-sync`, three-way TRUE, 15/15 files, service active); **822 tests green, coverage 76.2% ≥ 70** (was 75.85%), `ci/compile_all.py` clean; the `TestKillProcess` pair passes **with a stray `sleep` deliberately alive**; human-only items unchanged |
| 2026-09-12 | Buffy (live redeploy) | uncommitted fourth-review-batch set (running == checkout == installed, `handsoff.py` sha256 d0dc13d7…, `core/tools.py` b0b079cf…, `core/assistant.py` 6cc26705…, `core/calendar.py` bc22ec27…) | The six items left open by the earlier passes: **five fixed, one disproved, one new finding**, each pinned by a guard verified to fail on the unfixed code. (9) `watch_file` ReDoS — **measured, not assumed**: `(a+)+$` on a 29-char *non-matching* line runs for minutes (each character doubles it) on a thread nothing can interrupt, so the watcher would die for good; stdlib `re` has no timeout, so the exponential shape is refused, the pattern length is capped and the text per evaluation is bounded. My own first cut had a real bug the tests caught: it capped the lines examined but still advanced the cursor past the whole chunk, **silently dropping a large burst forever** — the cursor now advances only by the bytes examined, and the guard asserts bounded-per-poll AND nothing-skipped-in-order. (19) CONFIRM TOCTOU — **disproved**: the offer is read *and* cleared in one critical section, so a racing second confirm refuses; pinned with 8 real threads. The only thing the post-release read did was print a second log line, which is why it looked like a check-then-act, and that read is gone. Narrowing the offer to "only the next turn" was rejected — the turn marker is the generation counter, which other events bump, so it would sometimes refuse a legitimate confirm. (20) `BoundedJob` ×3: the drain buffer kept the **first** 200 KB, so `output_tail` ("recent output") returned the startup banner and dropped the error after it — it now keeps the newest bytes; a `SIGKILL` whose `wait` expired was swallowed and still reported `timeout-killed`, dropping the only reaper and leaving a zombie — it now stays running and retries; and a grandchild holding stdout kept the drainer blocked for the session — the reap path now releases it. (21) the notification reader's disable never joined (so "off" was only a promise and re-enabling ran two readers), and after five dead `dbus-monitor` respawns it left `notification_reader=True` with nothing listening — it now joins, and gives up loudly by persisting itself OFF. (22) pomodoro: unlocked check-then-act start (two loops, double announcements) now shares one re-entrant lock; the ghost transition — "pomodoro stopped" followed by "back to work" — is now impossible by re-checking under that lock at the phase flip; `shutdown()` joins. (23) ICS: `http://` was accepted for any host while the settings row advertised https, and a secret iCal URL is a bearer token; cleartext is now refused off-loopback (localhost still allowed) — and fixing it surfaced a **new leak**: the failure path echoed the source URL (HTTPError strings carry it) into the transcript, so sources are now reported scheme+host only. Deployed (`in-sync`, three-way TRUE, 15/15 files, service active; all four hashes matched); **841 tests green, coverage 76.45% ≥ 70** (was 76.22%), `ci/compile_all.py` clean; no new journal errors; human-only items unchanged |
| 2026-09-12 | Buffy (test-suite audit, no redeploy needed) | tests-only change (`tests/conftest.py` + 8 test modules; **no production file touched**, so the deployed bubble is unchanged and still `in-sync` at `handsoff.py` d0dc13d7…) | Whole-suite audit for the `TestKillProcess` class of defect — ambient system state, stray processes, ordering, wall-clock timing — fixed at the cause. **The systemic one: the suite graded itself against the developer's configuration.** The monolith reads `~/.config/handsoff/settings.json` at import and bakes it into `SETTINGS`/`SYSTEM_PROMPT`; this machine has `notification_reader: true`, so every `Assistant()` built anywhere in the suite spawned a live `dbus-monitor` and **13 tests leaked its reader thread**, and the prompt carried a personal assistant name. conftest now imports the app with `HOME`/`XDG_STATE_HOME`/`XDG_CONFIG_HOME` pointed at a throw-away directory (restored immediately) — the module is configured from defaults and the tests no longer read or write the real config, history, memory or control socket; a guard test asserts exactly that, because the failure mode is silent (everything still passes, just against someone else's settings). `test_hardening.py` was additionally loading its OWN second copy of the monolith, defeating the isolation and giving the suite two module objects with separate locks and caches — it now shares the one session-scoped instance. Two tests were **only passing because of the personal config**: the `cypher`→`Siphon` fuzzy-wake test inherited its name from the settings file (the pronunciation skeleton needs ≥4 letters sharing consonants, so the default name failed), and the prompt-identity test compared an import-time-baked `SYSTEM_PROMPT` against live settings. **Ordering is now irrelevant by construction:** autouse restore of the mutable module state means no test can be affected by one that ran earlier, and leaked stoppable worker threads (watcher, drainer, pomodoro, reader) now fail *the test that leaked them* after a bounded settle — verified to fire on deliberately leaked `watch-file`/`notification-reader` threads, and it found the 13 leaks above. Wall-clock bets replaced by condition polling: the four notify-coalescing tests slept 0.6–0.8 s against a 0.5 s debounce (now wait on the coalescer's own state + expected count, *stronger* — a trailing-duplicate mutation still fails); the `fake_ollama` fixture slept 0.8 s hoping the server had bound (now polls a TCP connect; an HTTP probe was written first and would have hung forever, since the fake only implements `POST /api/chat`); four sleeps-then-assert-a-worker-ran sites now poll via a shared `conftest.wait_for`; the `_bump_gen` contention probe slept 0.15 s and hoped (now wraps the lock so "nothing claimed yet" provably means blocked — verified to fail on an unlocked `_bump_gen`). A singleton whose occupant can DIE needs a third answer — "is it still alive?" and "may I take its slot?" must be one step — so `reserve()` gained a `reclaim` predicate, evaluated under the lock that hands the slot out, with the judged-dead occupant returned on the reservation (`.reclaimed`) for disposal outside it; a predicate that raises leaves the occupant where it was, and a failed reserve is not a refusal (nothing counted, nothing to cry wolf about). Two real improvements came with the move: the reader keeps its slot until its worker is genuinely gone (it used to clear `_thread` BEFORE the join, so a restart arriving during an unresponsive shutdown could still start a second reader beside the old one — a worker that outlives the join budget now keeps the slot and the predicate frees it when it dies), and the diagnostic refusal is durable evidence (`cap refusals: diagnostic-worker`), not just a sentence. The refusal sentence itself is now rendered by the registry (`refusal_line`), so the journal, `core/tools.py` and the doctor cannot drift into three accounts of one event — the pre-existing `"cap refusal: job at 4/4 held"` assertion is what pinned the wording through the move. Measured: two released-together enables started **1** reader (the loser answered "already on" — deliberately not logged as a cap refusal, since a repeated toggle is not a cap turning work away); 8 racing diagnostics produced **1 worker, 1 result, 7 refusals** recorded; **13/13 mutations of the new guards back to the old behaviour caught** (including "give the slot back before the slow part", "reclaim a live worker", "free the slot before the worker is gone", "never dispose of the corpse"). 988 tests green in six orderings (default, shuffled-test seeds 1/424242/deadbeef, shuffled-file seeds 2/cafef00d), coverage **78.26% ≥ 70**, `ci/compile_all.py` clean (40 files); deployed `in-sync` (three-way TRUE, 15/15 files, service active, `--ptt health` answering `ready` through the new `_diagnostic_call`, `--ptt status` answering); nothing committed |

No test could see it: every fixture had been written in the parser's own quoting style. New `core/web.py` (application-free, host-injected like `core/bubble.py`) owns a **probed router** over keyless backends — local SearXNG (auto-detected, preferred, probed by a localhost connect), DuckDuckGo, Stack Exchange, Hacker News, GitHub, Wikipedia — plus a **page reader** that fetches locally and says so (`via local fetch`) and falls back to Jina only when the local fetch is useless, naming that too (`via Jina Reader (third-party)`). Backends are chosen by deterministic regexes over the query (errors → Stack Exchange, repos → GitHub, news → the general pair), a failing backend **falls through** to the next and EVERY failure is named in the answer (`nothing found — stackexchange (Stack Exchange): quota exhausted, ddg (DuckDuckGo): HTTP 503`), one in-flight request per backend is admitted through the shared `core/registry.py` helper, and a TTL cache means a repeated question asks nobody twice. One tool was added (48 total), not five, because the fixed prompt is the scarce resource: `web_search(source, read_top)` gains the two-stage find-then-READ path, `read_page` is the new one, and `_fixed_prompt_tokens` was measured (6327 of 32768, both web schemas 265 tokens). **Everything the survey ruled out is as much the finding as what it kept**: Jina's search endpoint is 401 without a token, Mojeek captcha-walls scripts, and a public SearXNG instance (searx.be) answers a non-browser with Cloudflare's "Verifying your browser…" — so keyless general search means a LOCAL instance or a scrape. Doctor gained `search:` and `reader:` lines that report what this process has OBSERVED and say `untried` for a backend nothing has asked — never `ok` because it is configured, which is the false-positive shape found in the reference project's own web channel (its `check()` does no request at all). **Two real defects, both found by the live proof and neither by reading the code**: (1) DuckDuckGo serves an anti-bot page (`anomaly.js`, `cc=botnet`) after a burst of queries from one address, and that was being reported as **"no results"** — a blocked search and an empty web are different facts, so it now raises a named failure the router records and the answer speaks, with all three outcomes (results / honest empty / not-a-result-page) pinned; (2) the reader's fallback rule was "local text under 200 characters", so `example.com` — sixty readable characters — was being sent to a third party for nothing; a page is judged useless locally only when the site blocked us or when a LOT of markup carries almost no text (a JavaScript shell). `example.com` now reads `via local fetch`. The reader also REFUSES loopback, link-local, private-range and `.local` addresses — including a public name that RESOLVES to one — so a pasted or injected URL cannot read the router's admin page or a cloud metadata endpoint into the transcript. Delegating `_ddg_lite` / `_wiki_search` to the new backend immediately caught a regression a guard was holding: the briefing's five-item news feed had been cut to four by the router's own limit. **35/35 mutations** back to the old behaviour caught (three of the first sweep's misses were weak GUARDS, not bad mutations — a challenge fixture short enough that the thin-page rule alone triggered the fallback, a resolver guard that never checked the searxng setting, and a `lookup_fact` fake that answered the endpoint which made the delegation untestable); **1181 tests green** in three orderings (default, shuffled-test seed 20260913, shuffled-file seed 7); coverage **81.49% ≥ 70** with `core/web.py` at **93%**; `ci/compile_all.py` clean (45 files); deployed `in-sync` (installed == checkout; `CORE_REQUIRED` and the rehearsal's declared set gained `web.py`) and verified against the DEPLOYED module: Stack Exchange 2 real hits (`quota 291/300`), DuckDuckGo 2 real hits with real URLs, `example.com` read locally, a Reddit thread named as refusing automated readers, `http://192.168.1.1/` refused, doctor printing `search: searxng not running, ddg ok (2s ago), stackexchange ok (3s ago, quota 291/300), hn untried, github untried, wikipedia untried` — the user's own appearance and settings left untouched. Both docstrings claiming "memory and the file cannot disagree" were false. One new function answers "what does this value BECOME" — `coerce_setting(key, value)` completes the defaults with the value, coerces, and returns that key — and both `Settings.persist` and the bubble's `_persist_setting` now store it (unknown keys pass through untouched, so `set_setting` stays usable for a key this build has no rule for). **This is also why the tick paths the audit flagged as needing guards do NOT:** every `int()`/`float()` reader of SETTINGS (`mic_threshold`, `followup_seconds`, `engage_seconds`, `num_ctx`, `history_tokens`, the ram/vram/world/hardware values) reads a key `coerce_settings` rewrites, verified key by key against the coerter's own source — so with load AND both writers coercing, there is no longer a writer that can put junk there, and a guard on each reader would be defending a door that no longer opens. (2) *Rate limit.* `_tool_times` was `deque(maxlen=60)`, so a 61st stamp silently evicted the 1st: the schema allows 10 000 and the panel's spinbox 600, but every limit above 60 behaved as UNLIMITED while reading as enforced. It is now an unbounded deque whose window is enforced by TIME (the memory is already bounded — only calls the limit admitted are ever appended), and the limit itself is read by `_rate_limit()`, which falls back to the DEFAULT on junk the way the loader would. **`OverflowError` is in that guard for a measured reason:** a bare `Infinity` token in settings.json parses to a float, and `int(float('inf'))` raises it — the same crafted value `_num` was fixed to survive at load. (3) *ICS DURATION sign.* A leading `-`/`+` is RFC 5545's "this points BACKWARDS", and the chunk parser never saw the sign: `P-1D` came back as **+1 day** and `PT-5M` as **+5 minutes**, widening the overlap window instead of shrinking it. Refused rather than guessed (`-` or `+` anywhere in the token). (4) *Include-walk containment.* `str(inc).startswith(str(base))` admitted `/…/.config/niri-evil/x.kdl` as inside `/…/.config/niri` — a sibling directory fed text into the chord match. Now `inc.is_relative_to(base)` on resolved paths, with the base resolved and absolute includes resolved too. (5) *Decode cache.* `_IMAGE_CACHE_MAX` was 4 — the four state stills — while an animated pack names `PACK_MAX_FRAMES`=16 distinct files per state, so a 16-frame animation cycled 16 keys through 4 slots and **every frame advance re-decoded (load + scale + shade), several times a second, sustained** — a cost the still path never pays. Raised to 20 (`PACK_MAX_FRAMES + the four state stills`, so the animation stays resident while the OTHER states' entries rotate out) and pinned to that constant by a guard. (6) *Control socket read.* A fixed `recv(1024)` truncated `preview-pack <folder>` at 1024 bytes, so a long path **silently previewed a shorter, different folder** — no error, wrong art. Both clients half-close after sending, so the server now reads to EOF, bounded by `_CONTROL_REQUEST_MAX` = 65 536 (a denial-of-service ceiling, not a protocol limit) and by the existing 5 s timeout. (7) *Caps on the hand-editable lists*, the last of one rule: `spotter_models` 5, `workspace_aliases` 50, `command_policy` 64, `extra_allowed_commands` 64 with every entry normalised to a string (a JSON number or null in an allowlist is a matching surprise or dead weight). **14/14 mutations caught, zero residue**: the maxlen-60 deque restored, the rate-limit guard defanged twice (junk and Infinity each raised), each of the two writers storing the raw argument again, and the five caps removed one at a time. 1331 tests green in all three orderings (default, shuffled-test, shuffled-file); coverage **83.68% ≥ 70**; `compile_all` clean (45 files); `bash ci/gates.sh` green gate by gate. **The deployed tree is now BEHIND this checkout** — runtime bytes changed, so a redeploy is required before the fixes are live |

The Appearance tab had its own scroll area already; it now uses the same wrapper as the rest, so there is one implementation instead of two that can drift. **A second, independent defect in the picker itself:** `refresh_models` restored the selection from `cfg["model"]`, so a refresh landing after a pick put the STORED model back under the cursor and the next Save wrote the model that was already there. The row under the cursor IS the user's intent, so it is now read BEFORE the list is emptied and put back afterwards — reading it up front also sidesteps `QListWidget.clear()`, which was measured emitting `currentItemChanged` naming some OTHER item (clearing a list on row 2 emitted the item at row 1), so no handler may treat a rebuild as a choice. A stored model the server no longer has is no longer replaced by row 0, which used to make the next save quietly move off a model nobody touched. **Two more honesty fixes:** the status label wraps (a QLabel's minimum is the width of its text, and one save message asked for ~1680 px of window width), and a save that RAISES is now reported in the status label by every Qt entry point (`_save_reported`), because an exception escaping a slot goes to a stderr nobody sees when the window is opened from the bubble's menu — the report "I pressed Save and nothing happened", with nothing on screen and nothing in any log. **Guards:** three new scenarios — the window minimum must fit an ordinary 1280x720 screen WITH a long status line on it, the bar that saves must be inside the window on every tab, the tallest page must scroll rather than clip; a pick must survive a refresh and reach `settings.json`; and a failing save must say so and must not restart the bubble. **5/5 mutations caught, zero residue**, every restore sha256-verified (tabs unwrapped, the refresh restoring from `cfg`, the status label not wrapping, the row-0 fallback back, the save exception escaping). **1407 tests green in all three orderings** at the gate seed (default, shuffled-test, shuffled-file), coverage **84.24% ≥ 70**, compile/shell/smoke clean. **Deployed and verified on the INSTALLED bytes with the user's own settings and the real Ollama** (15 models): window minimum 331x173, all three bar buttons reachable at a 3072x1640 geometry, health line at y=1583, Permissions and Appearance scrolling, and picking `gemma4:26b` writing exactly that to `settings.json`; `deployment: in-sync`, `handsoff-settings.py` byte-identical between checkout and `~/.local/bin` |

The mechanism is in the turn path (`handsoff.py` ~6972): the history write sits behind `if` the turn's generation is still current, and the `else` branch logs `turn superseded at history-write; discarding` — so any capture that arrives while a turn is in flight silently discards the whole exchange. The visible consequence is the one the user complains about and does not connect to memory: `morning briefing delivered for 2026-09-18` fired **three times tonight** (19:50, 21:17, 21:24) because the once-a-day decision reads a history that never changes. Two fixes are implied and they are different: an interrupted turn should still persist what WAS said (or at least journal why it did not), and the briefing's once-a-day marker should not be a file that turns fail to write.

Two further facts reshaped the sweep: the blunt form of "reads count as writes" (drop the flags check for every path) used to be caught at collection and now dies earlier still, in the interpreter's start-up reads of the developer's user site-packages, so it proves nothing about this guard and the mutant is scoped to the checkout, where its evidence is the refusal text rather than a test assertion — reading is how the runner loads its own files. **Measured: the whole suite in the working tree 1 812 passed; the guard installed without the dirs; the child never told them; the shim ignoring what it was handed; the hook dropping the second rule; the boundary becoming a string prefix; the checkout losing its precedence; | 2026-09-19 | Buffy (the audit's own in-flight guard work had shipped a hang; the VRAM claim now counts what Ollama already holds; an edit that compiles but cannot load is rolled back) | `handsoff.py` d2c6b3929ead…, `core/tools.py` 07278a5a348c…, `tests/test_idle_release.py` afcb657bb9fc…, `tests/test_policy.py` 76801f9754cd…, `tests/test_lifecycle.py` 9b03d6d44d51…, `specs/20-architecture.md` 324ea0cf1d9a…, `specs/30-tools-api.md` 6f71bdf0986c… — **checkout only, NOT deployed:** `handsoff.py` and `core/tools.py` both DIFFER from `~/.local/bin/…`, so the running bubble is still the previous build | **The first thing this pass found was that the suite could not finish, and the cause was the audit's own fix.** `handsoff.py::_speak` read the streaming queue's `producer_alive` into a local and then never called it, so the empty-queue branch spun forever on a producer that had already died: `test_a_dead_producer_ends_the_wait` never returned, and the run had to be killed at 55% (~9 min in). The production shape is exactly audit bug #3 — the doctor says `thinking`, nothing is ever spoken, and only a manual barge-in ends it — and the guard that was supposed to prevent it was the half that hung. **Fixed at the cause:** the empty-queue branch now asks the queue's producer, and a producer that is gone gets ONE last non-blocking look for a sentence queued just before it ended (the timeout can expire in that window) before the loop finishes with what arrived and logs `reply stream ended without its terminator — speaking what arrived (N sentence(s))`. `_producer_still_here` answers **"still here"** when the probe raises and when the queue offers no probe at all: this is the second net behind `core.brain`'s guaranteed terminator, and a net that fired on an unreadable answer would truncate a reply, which is the failure it exists to prevent. Two other reds came from the same round and are fixed at the cause too: the restart note's new `and Path(exe).exists()` was both redundant (the validator already checks the configured script, absolute) and wrong for the bare-name invocation the validator explicitly accepts (`handsoff-restart` resolves on PATH, not in the working directory, so the note stayed unarmed), and `_reload_settings_live` reached `self._tools.set_permissions` unguarded, so a partially-built Assistant raised mid-reload with `SETTINGS` already replaced — both now read through `getattr`, and the ops cap test passes again. **FINDING 3 (recorded 2026-09-18, fixed here) — the VRAM claim now counts what the model already holds.** Worked out from the live numbers: the verdict asked for `7207 MB` (the `/api/tags` **blob** — what a turn would cost if it loaded the model from nothing, the right number for the doctor's `brain fit` question and the wrong one for a turn) while `gemma4:12b` sat **fully resident** (`/api/ps`: `size == size_vram`, 8.37 GB) and the card held 1 683 MB free with whisper and chatterbox holding 3 400 MB of it — so 1 683 + 3 400 = 5 083 free after a release, against 8 231 needed, and every turn logged `would not make room` for a model that needed **nothing**. `handsoff.py::_llm_need_mb(resident)` is the new requirement: `size − size_vram` (zero when entirely resident, the offloaded remainder when Ollama has split it, the fresh-load footprint when residency is unknown — never zero), and `_free_the_card_for_the_llm` asks residency **first** and returns `the LLM is already on the card in full, so this turn loads nothing and the voice was left alone` instead of a refusal. The doctor's `llm memory` line reads the same cached `need_mb`, so the sentence and the decision cannot disagree. **Teeth: two new guards in `tests/test_idle_release.py`** — the resident model with a blob that cannot fit a tight card (the exact live log line, asserted NOT to appear), and a SPLIT model (8.0 GB with 7.5 GB on the card) whose 512 MB remainder is what the release is spent on, which the blob claim would have refused. **Mutation-verified:** forcing `_llm_need_mb` back to the blob makes both fail with the live message reproduced verbatim (`8192 MB claim refused: 9216 MB needed (1024 MB reserve) but only 3900 MB is free`), and the restore was sha256-checked. **The audit's mistake #4 is closed too:** self/split `.py` edits were only `compile()`-checked, and the unit is `Restart=always`, so a module that raises at import never reaches `main()` and crash-loops the bubble until somebody runs `install.sh` by hand. `core/tools.py::_import_smoke` now imports the written file **in a subprocess, under the module's real name** (`core.tools` as `core.tools`, `handsoff.py` as `handsoff`, whose own canonical-name claim is satisfied because the import machinery registers it before the body runs), bounded at 60 s; on failure `_restore_previous` puts the `.bak` back (or removes a file that did not exist before) and `edit_file` returns `ERROR: … compiles but does not load — …` without restarting. **Teeth: three tests** — a `raise RuntimeError('boom at import')` self-edit is rolled back to its previous bytes, a loadable edit is kept, and a NEW file that cannot load is removed again. **Measured on the final bytes:** `tests` PASS — **1 845 passed in 257 s**; `coverage` PASS 257 s, **84.72%** (2 589 missing of 16 940) ≥ 70; `compile`/`shell`/`smoke` PASS; the generated spec tables regenerated from the code. **Stated limits:** `/api/ps` is now asked on EVERY turn (one localhost GET) because the claim cannot be computed without it — the earlier "ask residency only once the claim fails" rule is precisely what produced a refusal for a model that was already loaded; a bundle that cannot ask `/api/ps` keeps the fresh-load footprint rather than claiming zero; the smoke test costs a subprocess (~0.6 s for the app) and runs only on self/split `.py` writes; it proves the file LOADS, not that it behaves; (4) **The running bubble's own journal** — 3 402 lines, because a failure that already happened on this machine beats any amount of reading. **Four real defects, each with a guard that fails on the broken code.** **(a) The typing selftest's FAIL row named no reason:** `run_typing_selftest` read `str(out) if not err else str(out)` — BOTH ARMS THE SAME EXPRESSION — so a failed `type_text` landing check printed the text a SUCCESS produces and dropped the refusal that caused it: the one row a person reads when typing is broken said "typed …". `err` is a bool (the flag `ToolResult` carries), so the fix uses the tool's own text plus the `kind` that separates `refused` from `error`. **(b) A brain worker could write its result into the NEXT round's turn:** `_brain_turn` runs `for _round in range(MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS)` and assigns `turn`/`tools`/`box` once per pass, while both worker closures read those names from the enclosing scope — and a closure reads the NAME, not the value, so a worker that outlives its round writes `turn.result` into whatever object the next pass put there. Not hypothetical: the journal carries `stream worker slow to finish; waiting` twice (2026-09-18 21:18 and 21:23), and the code's own comment says the worker may still be finishing after 32 s. The PTT path already avoided this shape (`def _work(_rec=rec, _gen=gen, …)`); both brain workers now match it. **(c) A dead NaN term that read like a safety net:** `pack_animation` refused `not MIN <= fps <= MAX or fps != fps`, and the second term can never be the reason — every comparison against a NaN is False, so the bounds already refuse `nan` and `inf`. It matters because a manifest is somebody else's file and Python's `json` parses both `NaN` and `Infinity` into floats. **(d) A `global` with nothing to assign:** `_stop_recorder_bounded` declared `global _MIC_OPERATION_OWNER` while only the nested `_call` assigns it — a no-op that read as if the outer function owned the transition; and `search_note`/`reader_note` typed `now: float = None` while branching on `None`, an annotation that lied to every reader. **Teeth: 4 new tests** — `tests/test_ops.py::test_selftest_failure_names_the_reason` (drives the whole stage with a fake window world, asserts the reason is IN the row), a **third source-shape property** in `tests/test_regression.py::TestTheScansShapesAreCheckedProperties` (a `def` inside a loop that reads a name the loop assigns is refused unless bound as a default, with `scanned >= 3` so a sweep that finds nothing is a broken sweep rather than a passing one), `tests/test_design_packs.py::test_a_non_finite_fps_is_refused_by_the_bounds` (which also installs an IN-RANGE animation, so the check cannot pass by refusing everything), and a lambda in `tests/test_audio.py` whose loop variable is bound at definition. **Mutation sweep: 4/4 caught, 0 missed, every restore verified green.** **Refuted, with the mechanism, so nobody re-files them:** `cat ~/.config/handsoff/settings.json` IS allowed and that is by design — `read_file` refuses only `denied_secret_path` matches, settings.json is the app's own configuration, and it is WRITING it that is refused, by a different predicate — so the probe's expectation was wrong, not the validator (the probe itself was then deleted rather than left as a guard with a false premise); **`PLW1510`'s 45 sites already check the status at the call site** (every shipped one returns or examines `.returncode`, or raises on a non-zero exit in `hardware.cmd` — the rule flags the missing ARGUMENT, not the missing check); **the 13 `DTZ*` sites are local-time windows and displays that `ics_events_from_text` converts with `.astimezone()`** in its first three lines, so the naive-vs-aware comparison that would raise is unreachable, and the all-day DATE branch does the same before comparing; **the 56 `except Exception: pass` sites are teardown** (`t.cancel()`, closing a stream at shutdown, terminating a scratch process, a best-effort notification, a timing callback), read one by one rather than counted; and **the hardware prober's `niri_windows` returning a `CompletedProcess` where the others return a string is handled** — `_compositor` checks `.returncode` on exactly that object. **Measured on this tree:** `tests` gate **PASS — 1 919 passed in 256 s** (1 916 before, plus the 3 new); `order` gate **PASS** — seed shuffle **1 919 in 259 s**, file-order shuffle **1 919 in 262 s**; `coverage` **PASS, 85.29%** (2 542 missing of 17 275) ≥ 70; `compile` byte-compiled 54 files, `shell` and `smoke` PASS; `clean-checkout` **PASS (freshness 19 passed in 3.3 s)** with the module table regenerated (`handsoff.py` 9 022, `core/bubble.py` 4 076, `core/audio.py` 1 227) and three test-plan rows corrected (`test_ops.py` 76 → 77, `test_design_packs.py` 98 → 99, `test_regression.py` 151 → 152); `two-writer` **PASS**, the worktree unchanged through the run. **Stated limits:** the AST sweep judges a handler's `except` type and whether it logs, so a narrow handler that swallows something meaningful still passes; the closure rule is lexical and does not follow a worker's lifetime; the probe covered the command validator and the secret-path predicate, not `edit_file`'s path classifier (its own tests cover it); the two annotation fixes have no mutation behind them, because nothing at runtime can observe a type hint; and the journal evidence is this machine's 2026-09-18 session, so the "slow to finish" window is the SHAPE that was fixed rather than a reproduced lost turn. |

That is exactly the hole my own throwaway sweep had, and the reason its first `0 hits` was not trustworthy until a planted sample proved it could see anything at all. (2) **Comprehension scopes were not scopes** — `[lambda: i for i in range(3)]` is the classic late-binding error, `i` rebound per element, and the rule walked past it because a comprehension is not a `for` statement. Both fixed, and the fix is pinned by a **planted sample**, one shape per scope, whose exact offender LINES are asserted rather than their count — because the FIRST version of that assertion counted three offenders and was therefore satisfied by the two body shapes while the comprehension branch sat disabled, which a mutation proved by staying GREEN (2026-09-19). That is the same class of mistake the rule is about: a check that cannot fail is not a check. **Teeth: 4/4 mutations caught, 0 missed, every restore verified green** — the shipped brain worker unbound again, a SUITE lambda reading the loop's `seen` unbound again (the case that motivated the sweep), the rule reverted to body-only rebinding, and the comprehension branch disabled. **Measured on this tree:** `tests` gate **PASS — 1 920 passed in 256 s** (1 919 before, plus the new suite sweep); `order` **PASS** — seed shuffle **1 920 in 261 s**, file-order shuffle **1 920 in 263 s**; `coverage` **PASS, 85.29%** (2 542 missing of 17 275) ≥ 70; `compile` byte-compiled 54 files, `shell` and `smoke` PASS; `clean-checkout` **PASS (freshness 19 passed in 3.4 s)** with `specs/60-test-plan.md`'s `test_regression.py` row corrected to a real 153; `two-writer` **PASS**, the worktree unchanged through the run. **Stated limits:** the rule was lexical — it judged a closure by the names in its source, not by whether it outlives its iteration, so a `def` called immediately inside a loop was flagged too (the safe direction, and why the shipped workers are bound rather than exempted). **Two corrections to this row, made by the row below (2026-09-19):** there are **12** closures inside loops in the shipped tree, not "three subjects", and the PTT worker is not one of them — `_work` is defined in `finish_listening()`, not in a loop, so the rule has never judged it; the two brain workers are now pinned BY NAME. | 2026-09-19 | Buffy (the closure rule judges LIFETIME and READS the callee — an escape is flagged and named by the route it took, a call in its own iteration is left alone, and a helper that calls its closure in place stops being refused — even when it only passes the closure ON to another helper — while the planted sample stops being able to accept anything) | `tests/test_regression.py` 30e2ab1604c4…, `specs/60-test-plan.md` ca680117eaa7…, `specs/90-audit.md` 0e2ba59c8a2e… — **no deploy needed and none made** (no shipped file changed, so nothing on the card can move: `--ptt doctor` still reads `deployment: in-sync — installed copy matches the checkout`, and running `134c3c8661cc…` is `git show HEAD:handsoff.py` byte-for-byte) | **The row above ended on this limit in so many words** — "the rule is lexical, so it judges a closure by the names in its source, not by whether it actually outlives its iteration, so a `def` called immediately inside a loop is flagged too" — and a rule that demands a binding on code that cannot break is a rule people work around. The rule now asks the question its own docstring always claimed: **can this closure outlive its iteration?** Five routes out, each diagnosed in its own words (because one branch covering for another means one of them is dead): **stored** (`obj.cb = lambda: extra` → `stored on obj.cb`), **handed to a callee that keeps it** (`sink.append(lambda: item)` → `handed to append()`, `threading.Thread(target=lambda: item)` → `handed to Thread()`), **bound to a name that leaks** (`f = lambda: extra2` … `hold.append(f)` → ``bound to `f` and `f` is read as a value``; a named `def` returned by name → `` `worker` is read as a value ``), **collected** (a container literal holding it), and **a comprehension's element** (`[lambda: i for i in range(3)]` → one closure per element, `i` rebound as it runs). Left ALONE is the one shape that cannot outlive anything: a closure **called where it was written** — `(lambda: item)()`, `sorted(…, key=lambda k: item)` — because the names it closed over are still the loop's at that moment; the callees that consume what they are given are an explicit list (`CONSUMING`) and everything else is assumed to KEEP it, which is the safe direction (the cost of the assumption is an `x=x` default, the cost of the other is a worker writing into the next round's object). **The sample was lying, and the rule was fine.** The planted sample marked its escapes with trailing `# FLAG` comments — *Python comments*, not part of the string that gets parsed — so `CLOSURE_SAMPLE_FLAGS`, read back out of the text, was **EMPTY** while the rule worked perfectly: a sample with an empty expectation accepts ANY rule, which is the same class of failure the sample exists to catch. The verdict now travels WITH each line — a tuple of (source line, the word its reason must name) — and the assertion compares the flagged LINES, then asserts each line's REASON contains that word, then asserts every unmarked line was left alone, so a branch answering in another branch's wording fails and a rule that flags an in-place call fails. **A count floor was standing in for a subject.** The shipped half asserted `scanned >= 3` and its message called the subjects "the PTT worker and the two brain workers" — neither half survived measurement: the sweep judges **12** closures inside loops across the shipped files, and `_work` is defined in `finish_listening()`, NOT in a loop, so the rule has never judged the PTT worker at all (the idiom the rule came from was never one of its subjects). `_closure_offenders` now returns the closures it judged **by name**, the shipped half requires `_run_stream` and `_run_call` **by name** (measured: renaming one out of the sweep fails, and unbinding one is reported as `` `_run_stream` is read as a value `` — the route out, not merely an objection), and the suite half's floor moved 9 → 10 because one of its subjects lives in a comprehension, a scope the rule could not see two rounds ago. **And the callee is READ now, not assumed.** The rule knew a closure handed to `sorted` is consumed (a list of builtins) and assumed **every other callee keeps what it is given** — safe, and expensive: it demanded an `x=x` default from a helper that calls its closure and forgets it. `_definition_of` now resolves the call target by **unique name** in the same file — a `Name` (`take_it(...)`) or an `Attribute` (`Holder().commit(...)`, a METHOD, so positional arguments bind PAST `self`); `_parameter_for` binds the closure argument to the parameter it lands in and refuses to guess through a `*args` splat or a `*fns` parameter; `_consumes` walks the callee's body and requires **every use of that parameter to be a call in the callee's own body** — with `_directly_in` separating `def take_it(fn): fn()` (used during the call) from `def later(fn): Timer(1, lambda: fn()).start()` (called from a worker that outlives it). Anything unreadable — an external callee, a splat, an ambiguous name — keeps the old answer: assume it keeps it. **Measured reach:** 73 closures in this tree are passed to a call whose target is a unique def in the same file; **7 are called in place** (every one a `hardware._probe("section", lambda: …)` prober, and `_probe` does call `fn()` at line 156), 66 are kept or handed on, and 149 def names appear more than once in their file so they are never followed. None of the seven is currently a SUBJECT of the rule — they sit in `snapshot()`'s `try`, not in a loop — so this half is pinned by the sample today and is what the next such call site inherits. **And one level of indirection is read too.** `def through(fn): take_it(fn)` was still a false escape, because the parameter's use is an argument of another call rather than a call of it — so the rule now FOLLOWS a pass-through with the same code, from the callee's own body only (from `threading.Timer(1, lambda: take_it(fn))` the call outlives the wrapper and the refusal stands), refuses a wrapper whose target keeps the closure, and refuses a CYCLE of wrappers (`ping` → `pong` → `ping`) rather than descending for ever — the `chain` of (callee, parameter) pairs already walked is what makes a cycle a refusal. The sample grew four more shapes for it (`through` left alone, `relay` refused, `handoff` refused, `ping`/`pong` refused) plus `stash`, whose parameter is kept by an ASSIGNMENT rather than a call. **Measured in this tree: no such chain exists yet** — of the closures handed to a readable callee, 7 are called in place, 66 are kept or handed on and **0 arrive through a pass-through** — so this half is pinned by the sample today and inherited by the next wrapper. **Teeth: 18/18 mutations caught, 0 missed, every restore verified green** — 8 on the lifetime rule and its sample (every closure called in place; an in-place call treated as an escape; the stored-on branch answering with the handed-to word; the name-following branch killed; a sample verdict moved onto a line its reason no longer matches; a shape dropped from the sample; the shipped brain worker renamed out of the pinned subjects; and it unbound again) and 10 on the follow (the follow removed; every readable callee assumed to consume; a call from a nested worker read as in-place; an ambiguous name followed anyway; a method's argument bound WITHOUT skipping `self`; the first in-place call taken as consent with a later store ignored; a parameter kept by an assignment read as an in-place use; a wrapper that only passes the closure ON refused as a keeper; the cycle guard removed; and the `chain` not threaded into the recursion — the last two failing loudly as `RecursionError` rather than quietly, and one of the ten having been UNFALSIFIABLE until `stash` was added, because no sample shape kept a parameter without a call). **Measured on the final bytes:** `tests` **1 920 passed in 257.3 s**; `coverage` **85.29%** (2 542 missing of 17 275) ≥ 70; freshness **19 passed**. The collected count stays 1 920 because this round SHARPENED existing checks rather than adding a test — the suite's own file is measured, so the new lines are covered by the same run. | **Stated limits:** the follow resolves by unique name only, so a helper defined twice in its file is never followed, it reads the callee in the SAME file (an imported helper is assumed to keep its closure), a CYCLE of wrappers is refused rather than resolved (there is no fixed point to find, and refusing is the direction that cannot miss an escape), and a `*args`/`*fns` binding is refused rather than guessed — all of them conservative: a needless `x=x` default, never a missed escape; **the "reads one level, deliberately" limit this row used to carry is CLOSED by the paragraph above** — a parameter that is only ever passed on is now followed through; an escape through a name the loop does not rebind is still out of scope, because the rule only asks about names the loop rebinds; the suite half still covers `tests/*.py` only, not the two harness modules in `ci/`; and this round ran the suite, coverage and freshness on the final bytes but NOT the full gate set — the full set was run the next morning on the tree this round's work now sits in (all seven PASS), and the round was committed then; the hashes above are the bytes of that commit, recomputed because a machine restart lost the round's original scratch copies, and the suite count they imply (1 920) is the one that commit's own hook reproduced. |
| 2026-09-20 | Buffy (the closure rule follows a helper into its own module — an imported consumer stops being assumed to keep its closure, and the checkout root is enforced at the one place a file is read) | `tests/test_regression.py` 661a2214406b…, `specs/60-test-plan.md` 48b79b4440ef…, `specs/90-audit.md` 87ebd234d9e6… — **no deploy needed and none made** (no shipped file changed, so nothing on the card can move: `--ptt doctor` reads `deployment: in-sync — installed copy matches the checkout`, with running `134c3c8661cc…` = `git show HEAD:handsoff.py`) | **The row above ended on this limit in so many words** — it reads the callee in the SAME file, so a helper imported from another module is assumed to keep its closure — and the cost was a loop told to bind a name it can never read again, for a helper that in fact calls its closure in place. **How the follow crosses a file:** one parsed-module object (`_Source`) carries a tree, its OWN parent map and its path, and the parent map is load-bearing — `parents[node]` is only meaningful for the tree the node came out of, so the cross-file walk must use the target module's map (a mutation that uses the scanned file's map instead is caught, because the argument's use resolves to nothing and the callee is read as a keeper); `_module(path, root)` is the ONE place a file is read and the ONE place the root bound is enforced, so a path outside the checkout (or a name that only exists in an installed library) returns nothing and the call is not followed — bound once deliberately, because a second copy further out could never be falsified on its own; `_module_path` tries two homes, the importing file's own directory (`tests/` do `from conftest import …`, a package does `from .theme import …`) and the checkout root (`core/assistant.py` does `from core.registry import …`), and a RELATIVE import counts its levels up from the importing file's directory (`from ..harness import take_it` in `pkg/sub/deep.py` names `pkg/harness.py`); `_imports` builds `{name: (file, attribute or None)}` from every Import/ImportFrom — `import core.brain` binds both `core.brain` and `core`, `import harness as h` binds `h`, `from twice import tap` binds `tap` → `(twice.py, "tap")` — with a star import or a name that is no file here simply absent, which the caller reads as "assumed to keep what it is given"; and a dotted CALL name is tried longest first (`h.take_it` → the module, then `h`), so the most specific module wins. **That a name is imported is not yet an answer:** `from core.registry import BoundedRegistry` binds a CLASS, so a class, a constant or an imported lambda declines to be followed, exactly as a name defined TWICE in the imported module is refused rather than guessed — following the wrong body is how this rule would start missing escapes. **A hole found while writing it, in the unsafe direction:** the table is built from every Import node, so a name imported and then REBOUND at module level (`from harness import take_it` … `take_it = keep_it`) was still resolved to the imported body — a verdict about a function nobody calls, and the imported `take_it` consumes, so a call that actually runs a KEEPER would have been left alone as consumed. Names rebound at module level (assignment, annotation, or a module-level `def`/`class` of the same name) are now dropped from the table and the call falls back to assumed-kept, whose cost is a needless `x=x` default. **The sample is now real files parsed from real paths:** `test_an_imported_helper_is_read_instead_of_assumed` writes a package under `tmp_path` — `harness.py` (`take_it` calls its closure, `keep_it` stores it), `twice.py` (a `tap` defined twice) — and five consumers judged through the same helper the shipped sweep calls, so the machinery under test is the machinery that runs: the consumer of `take_it` must be left alone while the consumer of `keep_it` is flagged (measured `(2, [5])`), the ALIASED module resolves, the twice-defined name is not followed at all, the module-level REBOUND name is judged as kept (line 4), the RELATIVE import two levels up resolves, and the SAME consumer file judged with a root that does not contain the helper resolves NOTHING — both closures assumed kept — so the follow never leaves the root it was handed. **Measured reach in this tree: no such chain exists yet** — the shipped sweep judges **12** closures inside loops and resolves **0** call sites across a file boundary, so this half is pinned by the sample today and is what the next imported consumer inherits; what IS pinned in the shipped and suite sweeps is that the import table still reads this repository, the suite half requiring **≥ 5** `from … import …` names to resolve to a file in the checkout (measured: **151 of 185** bindings across 33 test files; the shipped tree 6 of 23 across 21 files) — a floor rather than a count, so a rename starves it visibly instead of silently — and the rebinding filter dropped nothing in either tree, which is itself a measurement (no module-level rebinding of an imported name exists here). **Teeth: 13/13 mutations caught, 0 missed, every restore verified green** — eleven on the cross-module half (the root bound dropped so a file outside the checkout is followed too; a relative import resolved against the root only; `import x as y` no longer binding `y`; an ambiguous name followed anyway; imported names never resolved at all; the follow dropping the root so no callee outside the file resolves; the cross-module walk using the SCANNED file's parent map; a name rebound at module level followed anyway; a wrapper that only passes the closure ON read as a keeper; the cycle guard removed, failing loudly as `RecursionError`; and a callee that calls its closure in place read as a KEEPER) plus two controls proving the refactor did not blunt the earlier verdicts (a shipped brain worker unbound again; comprehension scopes not scopes again). **Measured on the final bytes:** `tests` gate **PASS — 1 921 passed in 257 s** (1 920 before: this round adds the imported-consumer test); `order` **PASS**, seed shuffle **1 921 in 261 s** and file-order shuffle **1 921 in 260 s**; `coverage` **PASS, 85.29%** (2 542 missing of 17 275) ≥ 70; `compile`, `shell` and `smoke` PASS; `clean-checkout` **PASS** (freshness **19 passed in 3.2 s**); `two-writer` **PASS**, the worktree unchanged through the run; and `specs/60-test-plan.md`'s `test_regression.py` row moved 153 → 154. **Stated limits:** the import table is lexical, so a name imported and rebound INSIDE A FUNCTION is still resolved to the import — a stated hole, not dressed up; a star import is not read at all; a name defined twice in the imported module is refused rather than guessed; the same-named file in the importing file's own directory wins over the checkout root (the order this repository's two import styles require); everything outside the checkout is assumed to keep what it is given; and the shipped sweep's cross-module wiring has no subject yet, so the follow's live proof is the on-disk sample. |

| 2026-09-22 | Buffy | A stop-probe journal tripwire so the mystery restart killer gets CAUGHT next time — `handsoff-stop-probe` shipped beside `handsoff-restart` and wired as the unit's `ExecStop=`. Three injection turns lost their answer to a systemd stop (18:26, 18:30, 18:41–18:43) whose invoker the user journal never named: "Stopping handsoff voice assistant bubble…" is a stop JOB, so a post-hoc journal or /proc sweep is too late — the caller is gone once the job completes. `ExecStop=` is the one seam that runs while the invoker is still visible (a stop job blocks its caller, so whoever ran `systemctl --user stop/restart` — or the settings GUI's stop-the-unit path — is still sitting in /proc when the probe starts). The probe is read-only over /proc, appends one JSONL line to `~/.local/state/handsoff/stop-attribution.jsonl` (0600): the caller's pid, exe basename, cmdline (200 chars), and up to 6 hops of ancestry NEWEST-FIRST — the chain is what names the agent behind a `bash -c` wrapper. Unit-management clients only: the cmdline must mention handsoff AND be a systemctl/busctl/dbus-send/gdbus process; a python exe is excluded as a second net (the bubble's own cmdline names handsoff.py — the probe must never attribute a stop to the thing being stopped); an editor or journalctl tail merely mentioning handsoff is not a caller. `callers:[]` is itself information — what a session shutdown (PartOf=graphical-session.target) or a direct D-Bus call looks like. ExecStop runs ONLY on stop jobs: explicit stop, restart, session teardown — a crash respawn under Restart=always does NOT run it ("it died" and "someone stopped it" are different events, and the journal already covers the first). The probe spawns nothing, always exits 0 (a failing ExecStop marks the whole unit failed — a worse outcome than any missed attribution). Full installer wiring per the shipped-set rule: staged 0755, prev-save, switch 755, manifest hash (doctor now drift-detects the probe too), manifest-less uninstall fallback, unit ExecStop, `_DEPLOY_FILES` floor. Guards: six in `tests/test_lifecycle.py::TestStopProbe` — visible-caller attribution with its chain (EXECUTED against a fake /proc tree with symlinked exe entries, JSON-parse verified), bubble-never-a-caller (python exe excluded), editors/journal-tails-not-callers, empty-caller note + exit 0, 0600 append-only ledger, and installer-wiring pins (ExecStop=, stage/switch/rollback/uninstall/manifest). Two probe bugs fixed before they could fire: an undefined `$json_note_placeholder` in the final printf (dies under `set -u` → failed ExecStop → failed unit) and a chain-splice that emitted `"chain"` as a SIBLING of the caller object instead of a member (unparseable JSON — the ledger must be jq-able to be read); a failed ancestry hop consumes budget (a self-parented corrupt stat must never hang a stop job). CONSEQUENCE NAMED: the d34ebb5 pattern's first real fire was a false positive — its wording ("not a session shutdown") is wrong for the biggest session shutdown there is; fixed in b4c14bf (probe annotates exit.target sweeps, rule exempts them). STILL OPEN: the 18:26 / 18:30 / 18:41–43 mid-session stops of Sep 22 (boot -1, injection battle) remain unexplained — mid-session, machine up, no caller the journal could name; the tripwire exists for exactly these and the next occurrence will leave a name (or a definitive callers:[]) |
